Use the smallest responsible process.\n- \"This is simple, so I need the whole process anyway\" -> no. Tiny mechanical edits should stay tiny.\n- \"I'll create extra repository isolation just to be safe\" -> forbidden unless the user clearly asked for it.\n- \"I already know the skill\" -> read the current version before relying on it\n---\nname: systematic-debugging\ndescription: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n---\n\n# Systematic Debugging\n\n## Overview\n\nRandom fixes waste time and create new bugs. Quick patches mask underlying issues.\n\n**Core principle:** ALWAYS find root cause before attempting fixes. Symptom fixes are failure.\n\n**Violating the letter of this process is violating the spirit of debugging.**\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO FIXES WITHOUT ROOT CAUSE INVESTIGATION FIRST\n```\n\nIf you haven't completed Phase 1, you cannot propose fixes.\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse for ANY technical issue:\n- Test failures\n- Bugs in production\n- Unexpected behavior\n- Performance problems\n- Build failures\n- Integration issues\n\n**Use this ESPECIALLY when:**\n- Under time pressure (emergencies make guessing tempting)\n- \"Just one quick fix\" seems obvious\n- You've already tried multiple fixes\n- Previous fix didn't work\n- You don't fully understand the issue\n\n**Don't skip when:**\n- Issue seems simple (simple bugs have root causes too)\n- You're in a hurry (rushing guarantees rework)\n- Manager wants it fixed NOW (systematic is faster than thrashing)\n\n## The Four Phases\n\nYou MUST complete each phase before proceeding to the next.\n\n### Phase 1: Root Cause Investigation\n\n**BEFORE attempting ANY fix:**\n\n1. **Read Error Messages Carefully**\n   - Don't skip past errors or warnings\n   - They often contain the exact solution\n   - Read stack traces completely\n   - Note line numbers, file paths, error codes\n\n2. **Reproduce Consistently**\n   - Can you trigger it reliably?\n   - What are the exact steps?\n   - Does it happen every time?\n   - If not reproducible → gather more data, don't guess\n\n3. **Check Recent Changes**\n   - What changed that could cause this?\n   - Git diff, recent commits\n   - New dependencies, config changes\n   - Environmental differences\n\n4. **Gather Evidence in Multi-Component Systems**\n\n   **WHEN system has multiple components (CI → build → signing, API → service → database):**\n\n   **BEFORE proposing fixes, add diagnostic instrumentation:**\n   ```\n   For EACH component boundary:\n     - Log what data enters component\n     - Log what data exits component\n     - Verify environment/config propagation\n     - Check state at each layer\n\n   Run once to gather evidence showing WHERE it breaks\n   THEN analyze evidence to identify failing component\n   THEN investigate that specific component\n   ```\n\n   **Example (multi-layer system):**\n   ```bash\n   # Layer 1: Workflow\n   echo \"=== Secrets available in workflow: ===\"\n   echo \"IDENTITY: ${IDENTITY:+SET}${IDENTITY:-UNSET}\"\n\n   # Layer 2: Build script\n   echo \"=== Env vars in build script: ===\"\n   env | grep IDENTITY || echo \"IDENTITY not in environment\"\n\n   # Layer 3: Signing script\n   echo \"=== Keychain state: ===\"\n   security list-keychains\n   security find-identity -v\n\n   # Layer 4: Actual signing\n   codesign --sign \"$IDENTITY\" --verbose=4 \"$APP\"\n   ```\n\n   **This reveals:** Which layer fails (secrets → workflow ✓, workflow → build ✗)\n\n5. **Trace Data Flow**\n\n   **WHEN error is deep in call stack:**\n\n   See `root-cause-tracing.md` in this directory for the complete backward tracing technique.\n\n   **Quick version:**\n   - Where does bad value originate?\n   - What called this with bad value?\n   - Keep tracing up until you find the source\n   - Fix at source, not at symptom\n\n### Phase 2: Pattern Analysis\n\n**Find the pattern before fixing:**\n\n1. **Find Working Examples**\n   - Locate similar working code in same codebase\n   - What works that's similar to what's broken?\n\n2. **Compare Against References**\n   - If implementing pattern, read reference implementation COMPLETELY\n   - Don't skim - read every line\n   - Understand the pattern fully before applying\n\n3. **Identify Differences**\n   - What's different between working and broken?\n   - List every difference, however small\n   - Don't assume \"that can't matter\"\n\n4. **Understand Dependencies**\n   - What other components does this need?\n   - What settings, config, environment?\n   - What assumptions does it make?\n\n### Phase 3: Hypothesis and Testing\n\n**Scientific method:**\n\n1. **Form Single Hypothesis**\n   - State clearly: \"I think X is the root cause because Y\"\n   - Write it down\n   - Be specific, not vague\n\n2. **Test Minimally**\n   - Make the SMALLEST possible change to test hypothesis\n   - One variable at a time\n   - Don't fix multiple things at once\n\n3. **Verify Before Continuing**\n   - Did it work? Yes → Phase 4\n   - Didn't work? Form NEW hypothesis\n   - DON'T add more fixes on top\n\n4. **When You Don't Know**\n   - Say \"I don't understand X\"\n   - Don't pretend to know\n   - Ask for help\n   - Research more\n\n### Phase 4: Implementation\n\n**Fix the root cause, not the symptom:**\n\n1. **Create Failing Test Case**\n   - Simplest possible reproduction\n   - Automated test if possible\n   - One-off test script if no framework\n   - MUST have before fixing\n   - Use the `ultrapowers:test-driven-development` skill for writing proper failing tests\n\n2. **Implement Single Fix**\n   - Address the root cause identified\n   - ONE change at a time\n   - No \"while I'm here\" improvements\n   - No bundled refactoring\n\n3. **Verify Fix**\n   - Test passes now?\n   - No other tests broken?\n   - Issue actually resolved?\n   - If the bug was meaningful or systemic, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before declaring the fix complete.\n\n4. **If Fix Doesn't Work**\n   - STOP\n   - Count: How many fixes have you tried?\n   - If < 3: Return to Phase 1, re-analyze with new information\n   - **If ≥ 3: STOP and question the architecture (step 5 below)**\n   - DON'T attempt Fix #4 without architectural discussion\n\n5. **If 3+ Fixes Failed: Question Architecture**\n\n   **Pattern indicating architectural problem:**\n   - Each fix reveals new shared state/coupling/problem in different place\n   - Fixes require \"massive refactoring\" to implement\n   - Each fix creates new symptoms elsewhere\n\n   **STOP and question fundamentals:**\n   - Is this pattern fundamentally sound?\n   - Are we \"sticking with it through sheer inertia\"?\n   - Should we refactor architecture vs. continue fixing symptoms?\n\n   **Discuss with your human partner before attempting more fixes**\n\n   This is NOT a failed hypothesis - this is a wrong architecture.\n\n## Red Flags - STOP and Follow Process\n\nIf you catch yourself thinking:\n- \"Quick fix for now, investigate later\"\n- \"Just try changing X and see if it works\"\n- \"Add multiple changes, run tests\"\n- \"Skip the test, I'll manually verify\"\n- \"It's probably X, let me fix that\"\n- \"I don't fully understand but this might work\"\n- \"Pattern says X but I'll adapt it differently\"\n- \"Here are the main problems: [lists fixes without investigation]\"\n- Proposing solutions before tracing data flow\n- **\"One more fix attempt\" (when already tried 2+)**\n- **Each fix reveals new problem in different place**\n\n**ALL of these mean: STOP. Return to Phase 1.**\n\n**If 3+ fixes failed:** Question the architecture (see Phase 4.5)\n\n## your human partner's Signals You're Doing It Wrong\n\n**Watch for these redirections:**\n- \"Is that not happening?\" - You assumed without verifying\n- \"Will it show us...?\" - You should have added evidence gathering\n- \"Stop guessing\" - You're proposing fixes without understanding\n- \"Ultrathink this\" - Question fundamentals, not just symptoms\n- \"We're stuck?\" (frustrated) - Your approach isn't working\n\n**When you see these:** STOP. Return to Phase 1.\n\n## Common Rationalizations\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Issue is simple, don't need process\" | Simple issues have root causes too. Process is fast for simple bugs. |\n| \"Emergency, no time for process\" | Systematic debugging is FASTER than guess-and-check thrashing. |\n| \"Just try this first, then investigate\" | First fix sets the pattern. Do it right from the start. |\n| \"I'll write test after confirming fix works\" | Untested fixes don't stick. Test first proves it. |\n| \"Multiple fixes at once saves time\" | Can't isolate what worked. Causes new bugs. |\n| \"Reference too long, I'll adapt the pattern\" | Partial understanding guarantees bugs. Read it completely. |\n| \"I see the problem, let me fix it\" | Seeing symptoms ≠ understanding root cause. |\n| \"One more fix attempt\" (after 2+ failures) | 3+ failures = architectural problem. Question pattern, don't fix again. |\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n---\nname: building-react-apps\ndescription: Use when building, refactoring, planning, or debugging React or Next.js apps, React components, hooks, routing, frontend state, SSR, hydration, bundles, imports, or UI settings\n---\n\n# Building React Apps\n\nUse these defaults for React app work unless the user or existing codebase explicitly chooses another architecture.\n\nIf the work is specifically about slowness, root-load time, compile time, SSR effectiveness, hydration cost, bundle size, import bloat, or decluttering heavy UI dependencies, use `optimizing-react-next-apps` as the focused workflow.\n\nIf the app has server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, persisted runtime state, or client/server synchronization, use `client-server-state-invariants` before planning or changing the state model.\n\n## UI Starting Point\n\n- For new React app screens, forms, and major surfaces, use `shadcn/ui` unless the user or existing app has chosen another design system.\n- Before inventing layout, inspect the available ShadCN Blocks at https://ui.shadcn.com/blocks for the specific screen or form category.\n- Start from the closest matching block with `npx shadcn add <block-name>` or the official source in https://github.com/shadcn-ui/ui, then add, remove, and adapt controls to fit the product.\n- Do not use a blank custom layout as the first implementation path when a relevant ShadCN Block exists.\n- Before adding any individual control, check whether `shadcn/ui` already provides a suitable control. Use/customize the shadcn control before hand-building a one-off control.\n\n## State And Data\n\n- Always centralize React app state in global single-source-of-truth Manager classes.\n- Components, hooks, and listeners subscribe to Manager updates and use Manager methods to update data.\n- Centralized state does not mean broad subscriptions. Components must subscribe to the smallest slice they need, preferably with selector hooks and equality checks, so unrelated UI does not re-render.\n- Never let app shells, page roots, sidebars, timelines, or other broad containers subscribe to an entire high-churn Manager snapshot when they only need stable fields. Draft text, cursors, hover/open state, search text, and other per-keystroke or per-pointer state need narrow component-level subscriptions.\n- If typing in an input, moving a cursor, toggling a hover/open flag, or editing a small draft makes the whole app repaint, the subscription boundary is wrong even if the data source is centralized.\n- Always implement a custom data manager for any app data structure.\n- Never use separate state variables or arrays as the source of truth.\n- Never send data around in transactions. Pass intent, ids, commands, or Manager method calls instead.\n\n## React Control Flow\n\n- Avoid using `useEffect()` to update state. Prefer `useCallback()` handlers or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.\n- Work ahead of time to avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.\n- When debugging React and a fix does not work, explicitly consider whether the root cause is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.\n- Every async callback, mutation result, timer, poll, or stream message that can update visible state must prove it still owns the current context before mutating Managers or navigation.\n- Use `RefObject` instead of `MutableRefObject`.\n\n## Routing And Modules\n\n- Prefer explicit routing over file-based routing when the framework does not strongly default to file-based routes. Framework-native file routing, such as Next.js App Router, is fine.\n- Never use `require()` to import modules. Use `import` instead.\n- Avoid `npm` by default. Use `pnpm` for package installation, scripts, and lockfiles unless the user or existing project explicitly chooses another package manager.\n\n## Next.js And SSR Defaults\n\n- Treat `\"use client\"` as a boundary with a cost. Do not put an entire page, app shell, sidebar, timeline, composer, settings surface, and data boot path behind one giant client component unless there is a deliberate reason.\n- SSR is only useful if the initial HTML contains meaningful above-the-fold UI or data. If `curl` for the route only shows a boot shell, script payloads, or empty placeholders, the route is not meaningfully server-rendered even if SSR is technically enabled.\n- Server-render the first useful screen by fetching route-stable bootstrap data on the server: auth/session state, settings needed to choose the shell, selected route ids, and initial page/snapshot data. Hydrate Managers or query caches from that bootstrap data; do not wait for client `useEffect()` plus client fetches before the first meaningful paint.\n- Use client effects for browser-only concerns: localStorage-only preferences, notifications, SSE/WebSocket subscriptions, focus management, and post-paint refreshes. Do not use effects as the only path for data required to decide what the initial screen is.\n- In Next dev, first request time includes compilation. Distinguish cold compile cost from runtime slowness before optimizing the wrong layer.\n\n## Import And Bundle Hygiene\n\n- Keep imports proportional to the UI being rendered. Do not import an entire complex surface for a tiny control; split shared tiny controls into their own files instead of importing them from heavy feature modules.\n- Lazy-load rarely opened or route-conditional surfaces with dynamic imports: settings dialogs, side panels, file viewers, terminals, worker inspectors, onboarding flows, QR/pairing dialogs, and large markdown/code viewers.\n- Avoid package and barrel imports that drag in whole icon packs, design systems, syntax highlighters, markdown stacks, charting libraries, or component suites for one glyph or one primitive. Inline small SVG paths or use local assets when that is cheaper and stable.\n- Prefer native browser controls for small, ordinary selects and pickers. Do not import a popover/dialog/menu stack for a low-value dropdown unless search, virtualization, or rich item rendering is genuinely needed.\n- Use subpath imports when a package supports them. Avoid root package imports from large UI libraries if a narrower entrypoint exists.\n- After dependency or UI-shell changes, inspect the emitted dev chunks or traces. Verify whether heavy modules moved out of the root chunk rather than assuming dynamic import worked.\n\n## Performance Debugging\n\n- Start with evidence: dev server logs, route timings, emitted chunk sizes, module counts, and initial HTML content.\n- Separate long-lived requests from slow requests. SSE, streaming, and hanging fetches can show huge durations in logs without blocking page paint.\n- Check recent dependency additions first when compile time suddenly explodes. A single icon, markdown, chart, or UI package can add thousands of modules.\n- For Next.js root-load complaints, answer three questions before changing code: what compiled, what HTML was returned, and what client-side requests gated the first useful paint?\n\n## Frontend Configuration\n\n- Never store frontend string literals or UI settings in `.env` files.\n- Put frontend strings and settings in normal source files, inline arrays, or `.json` files so they remain reviewable, testable, and versioned with the UI.\n\n## Internationalization\n\n- For meaningful React UI work, offer language support as an option when the user has not already decided: \"Should we support other languages? If yes, I will wire user-facing copy through i18n resources now instead of hardcoding strings.\"\n- If the user says yes, asks for translation/i18n/language/locale support, or reports that language switching only updates part of the UI, use `implementing-react-i18n`.\n- Use the i18n library's `t(\"key\", params?)` function for every user-facing string. Use `useI18nSnapshot()` only to subscribe React components so language changes repaint.\n- Never name variables with locale codes or locale-like names such as `it`, `en`, `enUS`, `locIt`, or `locEnUs`.\n\n## Settings UI\n\n- For settings dialogs, preferences panels, configuration modals, option tabs, or UI settings surfaces, use `designing-settings-dialogs`.\n---\nname: agentic-user-journey-testing\ndescription: Use when validating app, UI, or product-surface work through the running app, especially when logs/tests pass but a user job may still be blocked or unclear\n---\n\n# Agentic User Journey Testing\n\n## Overview\n\nAn app is not done because logs are quiet. It is done when a user can complete the intended job through the running interface.\n\nAgentic user journey testing is a black-box verification mode: give an agent only the running app, an interface to operate it, and a user mission. If the agent cannot complete the mission, the product is not yet usable for that story.\n\n## Approval Gate\n\nThis test mode can spend significant tokens and time.\n\nDo not run an agentic user journey test unless the user explicitly approves it or directly asks for it.\n\nWithout approval, you may:\n\n- identify candidate journeys,\n- write the mission prompts,\n- explain what the test would prove,\n- add deterministic tests that support the same behavior.\n\nBefore running, ask for approval and name:\n\n- the user mission,\n- the running app URL or entry point,\n- the expected visible completion state,\n- the estimated cost or scope in plain language.\n\nIf approval is not given, say the agentic journey test was planned but not run.\n\n## When To Use\n\nUse for app, UI, and product-surface work where user success depends on more than a single function or endpoint.\n\nEspecially use when:\n\n- logs show no errors but the visible app state may be wrong,\n- the UI may be confusing even if the code path works,\n- a workflow spans multiple screens, statuses, or async states,\n- loading, unread, waiting, completed, or error states matter,\n- the task is about whether a real user can accomplish a job.\n\n## Test Contract\n\nEach test needs a mission card:\n\n- **Running app:** URL, route, or entry point.\n- **User role:** who the agent is pretending to be.\n- **Mission:** the job to accomplish in user language.\n- **Allowed interface:** browser, app UI, keyboard, pointer, accessibility tree, or other user-level controls.\n- **Forbidden shortcuts:** no source inspection, no backend logs, no database queries, no implementation knowledge unless the user explicitly allows it.\n- **Expected completion proof:** the visible state that proves the job is complete.\n- **Failure conditions:** places where \"no error\" still counts as failure.\n\nExample:\n\n```text\nRunning app: http://localhost:3050/session/<id>\nUser role: operator returning to a direct-control session\nMission: determine whether the CLI is waiting for input and clear the unread state by reading it\nAllowed interface: browser UI only\nExpected completion proof: unread state appears until read; after reading, there is no spinner and no unread dot\nFailure conditions: spinner keeps spinning, no visible status changes, user cannot tell what to do, or the app silently does nothing\n```\n\n## How To Run\n\nUse the current harness's browser or UI-control tools. Behave like a user:\n\n1. Open the app at the provided entry point.\n2. Inspect only what a user could perceive.\n3. Decide where to click or type from the interface itself.\n4. Attempt the mission.\n5. Record visible evidence of success or the exact point of failure.\n\nDo not treat clean logs, HTTP 200 responses, or lack of console errors as success. Those are supporting signals only.\n\n## Pass Criteria\n\nThe journey passes only when:\n\n- the agent can understand what to do from the UI,\n- the agent can complete the mission through user-level actions,\n- the visible app state matches the expected completion proof,\n- no stale loading, misleading status, silent no-op, or hidden failure remains.\n\n## Failure Means Work Remains\n\nIf the journey fails, fix the product or the supporting state model. Do not dismiss the failure as \"just the test agent being confused\" unless there is clear evidence the mission was malformed.\n\nCommon product failures:\n\n- the app is technically idle but still shows running state,\n- a click succeeds internally but nothing visible changes,\n- the user cannot discover the next action,\n- the UI hides the real error,\n- the completion state is ambiguous.\n\n## Relationship To Other Tests\n\nAgentic journey tests complement deterministic tests. They do not replace:\n\n- unit tests,\n- integration tests,\n- state-machine tests,\n- accessibility checks,\n- deterministic Playwright assertions.\n\nWhen a journey fails, add deterministic regression coverage for the underlying behavior whenever practical.\n"
  }
]
````

3a9036b5b07ee56511b669ee7575f29ff029fa2e\nAuthor: Daniel <masterman@MiniMax.local>\nDate:   Mon Jun 15 16:02:21 2026 +0200\n\n    Add interactive terminal panel to the UI\n    \n    Real pty-backed terminal embedded in the web UI:\n    - TerminalManager spawns node-pty sessions with a bounded output ring\n      buffer (resumable over SSE) and idle/disconnect reaping; resolves cwd\n      server-side from the conversation's run record.\n    - HTTP routes: POST /api/terminals (spawn), GET /:id/stream (SSE),\n      POST /:id/input, POST /:id/resize, DELETE /:id.\n    - InteractiveTerminal (xterm + FitAddon) streams output over SSE and\n      POSTs keystrokes/resizes back.\n    - Desktop: resizable vertical pane split from the conversation, toggled\n      by a SquareTerminal icon in the header. guards, memory tools, project settings, runs lookup | Migrated |\\n     1\\t# Runner deployment and operations\\n     2\\t\\n     3\\t## Process model\\n     4\\t\\n     5\\tOne OmniHarness runner is one logical deployment with two co-located\\n     6\\tprocesses:\\n     7\\t\\n     8\\t- the API server owns SQLite, auth, supervision, PTYs, settings, files, and the\\n     9\\t  HTTP/SSE contract;\\n    10\\t- the ACP bridge owns agent processes and listens on loopback by default.\\n    11\\t\\n    12\\tClients connect only to the API server. Electron, iOS, Android, VS Code, and\\n    13\\tbrowser/PWA clients never connect directly to the bridge.\\n    14\\t\\n    15\\t`pnpm runner` starts the API server and starts or adopts a loopback bridge. The\\n    16\\tAPI remains available in a degraded state while a failed bridge retries. All\\n    17\\tstart, adopt, retry, refusal, failure, and shutdown choices emit named events.\\n    18\\t\\n    19\\t## Build and start\\n    20\\t\\n    21\\tBuild the shared static interface and start a normal runner:\\n    22\\t\\n    23\\t```bash\\n    24\\tpnpm install\\n    25\\tpnpm build\\n    26\\tpnpm runner\\n    27\\t```\\n    28\\t\\n    29\\tThe default API/UI address is `http://0.0.0.0:3050`; the managed bridge defaults\\n    30\\tto `http://127.0.0.1:7800`.\\n    31\\t\\n    32\\tPort `3050` is also the existing public-tunnel origin contract. Replacing the\\n    33\\tweb framework or static-interface implementation does not authorize changing\\n    34\\tthat deployed port or the configured public hostname. During a cutover, start\\n    35\\tthe replacement runner on the existing origin first and verify the public\\n    36\\thealth endpoint before changing any tunnel route.\\n    37\\t\\n    38\\tSupported runner options:\\n    39\\t\\n    40\\t```text\\n    41\\t--host <address>\\n    42\\t--port <0-65535>\\n    43\\t--bridge-url <url>\\n    44\\t--static-dir <directory>\\n    45\\t--no-static\\n    46\\t```\\n    47\\t\\n    48\\tThe equivalent environment settings are:\\n    49\\t\\n    50\\t```text\\n    51\\tOMNIHARNESS_RUNNER_HOST\\n    52\\tOMNIHARNESS_RUNNER_PORT (PORT is a compatibility fallback)\\n    53\\tOMNIHARNESS_BRIDGE_URL\\n    54\\tOMNIHARNESS_STATIC_DIR\\n    55\\tOMNIHARNESS_NO_STATIC=1\\n    56\\tOMNIHARNESS_MANAGE_BRIDGE=0\\n    57\\t```\\n    58\\t\\n    59\\tWith the default build, the runner serves `dist/interface`. `--no-static` is a\\n    60\\tsupported API-only mode. If the default artifact is absent, startup continues\\n    61\\tand emits `runner.static_ui_missing`; if an explicitly supplied `--static-dir`\\n    62\\tis missing or invalid, startup fails. Never combine `--no-static` and\\n    63\\t`--static-dir`.\\n    64\\t\\n    65\\t## Instances, data, and locks\\n    66\\t\\n    67\\t`OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` selects the base data root. `OMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE=name`\\n    68\\tplaces one isolated deployment under\\n    69\\t`$OMNIHARNESS_ROOT/instances/name`. Each instance owns:\\n    70\\t\\n    71\\t```text\\n    72\\tsqlite.db\\n    73\\t.omniharness-auth.key\\n    74\\trunner.lock.json\\n    75\\tbridge.lock.json\\n    76\\tapp-data/run-data/\\n    77\\t```\\n    78\\t\\n    79\\tThe SQLite database and worker JSONL files are one consistency domain. Do not\\n    80\\tpoint two live runners at the same instance root. A second process refuses an\\n    81\\towned runner lock. Separate instances need separate API ports and bridge URLs.\\n    82\\t\\n    83\\t## TLS and reverse proxies\\n    84\\t\\n    85\\tThe Node runner currently terminates HTTP. For any non-loopback deployment,\\n    86\\tterminate HTTPS at a reverse proxy or private tunnel and expose only the API/UI\\n    87\\tport. Keep the ACP bridge on loopback.\\n    88\\t\\n    89\\tForwarding headers are ignored unless the socket peer matches an explicit\\n    90\\tcomma-separated `OMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES` rule. Rules may be exact IPs or\\n    91\\tIPv4 CIDRs:\\n    92\\t\\n    93\\t```text\\n    94\\tOMNIHARNESS_TRUSTED_PROXIES=127.0.0.1,10.20.0.0/16\\n    95\\tOMNIHARNESS_PUBLIC_ORIGIN=https://runner.example.net\\n    96\\t```\\n    97\\t\\n    98\\tConfigure the proxy to replace, not append untrusted client values for,\\n    99\\t`Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*`. Never use a catch-all trusted-proxy range.\\n   100\\t\\n   101\\t## Backup and restore\\n   102\\t\\n   103\\tStop the runner before a filesystem backup. Copy the entire instance directory,\\n   104\\tincluding the database, auth key, worker streams, locks, settings, and managed\\n   105\\tmetadata. Exclude only transient build caches. Also back up the deployment's\\n   106\\tpassword-hash configuration separately.\\n   107\\t\\n   108\\tRestore into an empty instance path while the runner is stopped, preserve file\\n   109\\tpermissions, then start it with the same `OMNIHARNESS_ROOT` and\\n   110\\t`OMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE`. The auth key is required to read encrypted settings and\\n   111\\tvalidate existing sessions. If it is intentionally omitted, rotate the\\n   112\\tpassword and expect every client to authenticate again.\\n   113\\t\\n   114\\tClient profile lists and platform-secure bearer sessions are client state, not\\n   115\\tpart of a runner backup.\\n   116\\t\\n   117\\t## Disposable smoke check\\n   118\\t\\n   119\\tThis uses an isolated root and a hidden prompt. The password is not passed as a\\n   120\\tcommand-line argument or printed:\\n   121\\t\\n   122\\t```bash\\n   123\\tsmoke_root=\\\"$(mktemp -d \\\"${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/omniharness-smoke.XXXXXX\\\")\\\"\\n   124\\ttrap 'test -n \\\"$smoke_root\\\" && rm -rf -- \\\"$smoke_root\\\"' EXIT\\n   125\\tread -r -s -p \\\"Temporary runner password: \\\" smoke_password\\n   126\\tprintf '\\\\n'\\n   127\\texport OMNIHARNESS_AUTH_PASSWORD=\\\"$smoke_password\\\"\\n   128\\tunset smoke_password\\n   129\\tOMNIHARNESS_ROOT=\\\"$smoke_root\\\" \\\\\\n   130\\tOMNIHARNESS_INSTANCE=smoke \\\\\\n   131\\tpnpm runner --host 127.0.0.1 --port 3059 --no-static\\n   132\\t```\\n   133\\t\\n   134\\tIn another terminal, check liveness with:\\n   135\\t\\n   136\\t```bash\\n   137\\tcurl --fail --silent --show-error http://127.0.0.1:3059/api/healthz\\n   138\\t```\\n   139\\t\\n   140\\tPress Ctrl-C in the runner terminal. The trap removes only the temporary root\\n\\nPRODUCT\\n     1\\t# Product\\n     2\\t\\n     3\\t## Register\\n     4\\t\\n     5\\tproduct\\n     6\\t\\n     7\\t## Users\\n     8\\t\\n     9\\tSoftware developers and technical leads supervising several coding agents across local, remote, desktop, editor, and mobile surfaces. They work in long-running implementation and debugging sessions where clear state, rapid switching, durable recovery, and trustworthy control matter more than decorative presentation.\\n    10\\t\\n    11\\t## Product Purpose\\n    12\\t\\n    13\\tOmniHarness is a durable control plane for starting, observing, steering, and recovering ACP-backed coding-agent work. Success means a user can understand what every runner and worker is doing, take the next safe action quickly, move between devices and servers without losing context, and trust that failures, credentials, and recovery states are represented honestly.\\n    14\\t\\n    15\\t## Brand Personality\\n    16\\t\\n    17\\tCalm, capable, direct. The interface should feel like a precise professional tool with a human voice: dense when the work demands it, quiet when it does not, and explicit about uncertainty or failure.\\n    18\\t\\n    19\\t## Anti-references\\n    20\\t\\n    21\\t- Generic analytics dashboards built from interchangeable metric cards.\\n    22\\t- Sci-fi command centers with neon decoration, fake telemetry, or gratuitous motion.\\n    23\\t- Chat interfaces that hide execution state and recovery controls behind conversational chrome.\\n    24\\t- Consumer onboarding that stretches a simple connection task into a promotional sequence.\\n    25\\t- Interfaces that make remote, offline, authentication, or identity failures look like temporary loading.\\n    26\\t\\n    27\\t## Design Principles\\n    28\\t\\n    29\\t1. Show authority and scope. Every action, status, notification, and cache belongs visibly to one runner and one conversation.\\n    30\\t2. Keep supervision fast. Frequent actions remain close to the active work, with familiar controls and strong keyboard behavior.\\n    31\\t3. Make failure honest and recoverable. Name the affected runner, explain the state, and offer the smallest safe next action.\\n    32\\t4. Preserve context across time and surfaces. Switching, reconnecting, and returning later should not erase drafts, cursors, or the user’s mental model.\\n    33\\t5. Let the work dominate. Navigation and status remain legible but visually restrained around agent output, terminal content, and decisions.\\n    34\\t\\n    35\\t## Accessibility & Inclusion\\n    36\\t\\n    37\\tTarget WCAG 2.2 AA. All flows must support keyboard navigation, visible focus, screen readers, zoom and narrow layouts, live language changes, reduced motion, and status communication that does not rely on color alone.\\n\"\ndocs/codex-history/conversations/2026/07/2026-07-31-codex-conversation-019fb894-019fb894-4ac8-7063-b576-4410d57dbbf6-part-001.md:896:const r = await tools.exec_command({\"cmd\":\"pnpm \"last_agent_message\": \"Fixed the truncation cause. The worker tail endpoint was returning “last 100 rows,” but long agent answers can be stored as hundreds of tiny message rows, so the UI could receive only the back half of one answer. Now [output-store.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/output-store.ts:1341) detects that case and expands the tail back to the start of the streamed answer before rendering.\\n\\nI also added a regression test at [output-store.test.ts](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/server/workers/output-store.test.ts:281) and recorded the lesson at [tail-windows-must-not-split-streamed-messages.md](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/superpowers/learnings/2026-07-09-tail-windows-must-not-split-streamed-messages.md:1). While verifying, I also fixed the worker entries hot path so read-only legacy stream loads do not pull in database modules unnecessarily.\\n\\nVerified:\\n- `pnpm typecheck`\\n- `pnpm test tests/server/workers/output-store.test.ts`\\n- `pnpm test tests/api/worker-entries-hot-path.test.ts`\\n- `pnpm test tests/lib/agent-output.test.ts tests/app/worker-entries-manager.test.ts`\",\n"

tools.exec_command({\n350:    \"text\": \"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 18019)\\nTotal output lines: 1156\\n\\n# Complete Claude Code and ACP 0.25 Client Design\\n\\n**Date:** 2026-07-11\\n**Status:** Approved for implementation\\n\\n## Goal\\n\\nMake OmniHarness a complete, truthful ACP client. Claude Code must never block on an interaction the conversation cannot display, and no ACP 0.25 request, notification, session update, capability, or content type may be silently dropped.\\n\\n## Incident\\n\\nSession `0abd06014635` emitted a valid Claude Code `AskUserQuestion` tool call followed by an ACP form elicitation containing selectable answers. The runtime persisted both records, but the main conversation renderer ignored `elicitation` entries. The only actionable form lived in the optional worker sidebar. The conversation therefore appeared stuck while the agent correctly waited for input.\\n\\nThe audit found a protocol-version mismatch behind this partial implementation: OmniHarness is compiled against `@agentclientprotocol/sdk@0.14.1`, while the installed Claude adapter uses ACP SDK `0.25.0`. Elicitation was manually routed through `extMethod`, and several newer session updates and capabilities were never modeled.\\n\\n## Users and Jobs\\n\\nThe primary user is a human supervising coding agents from the main conversation.\\n\\n- When an agent asks a question, the user answers at the exact point where execution paused.\\n- When an agent requests permission or a mode change, the user sees the operation, every offered decision, and its consequences.\\n- When an agent emits plans, tasks, terminal sessions, diffs, commands, media, resources, or session changes, the user can inspect the real state without opening a debugging panel.\\n- After reload, reconnect, server restart, or worker recovery, pending actions remain actionable exactly once.\\n- Operators and tests can inspect every protocol decision through named events and persisted streams.\\n\\n## Product Principles\\n\\n1. **The conversation is the primary control surface.** Worker sidebars may mirror actions, but they may not be the only place to continue a blocked turn.\\n2. **Advertised means operational.** OmniHarness advertises a capability only when its request handling, persistence, user/API surface, error behavior, and tests are complete.\\n3. **Unknown means visible failure.** Unknown standard ACP methods or session updates emit typed compatibility errors; they are never ignored.\\n4. **One persisted worker stream.** Protocol output and human-input state remain in the existing append-only worker JSONL. No parallel transcript store is introduced.\\n5. **Stable identity and exactly-once decisions.** Requests use stable session/request/tool-call identifiers. Replays and duplicate responses cannot answer a different or already-settled request.\\n6. **Protocol data is stored, UI copy is translated at render time.** No translated strings are persisted.\\n\\n## Protocol Baseline\\n\\nOmniHarness will compile against `@agentclientprotocol/sdk@0.25.0`, matching the Claude adapter. The dependency is pinned to an exact compatible version and covered by a schema contract test.\\n\\nThe compatibility gate reads the installed SDK schema and asserts that every standard ACP 0.25 method, notification, session-update discriminator, content type, and advertised capability is classified as one of:\\n\\n- fully handled and advertised;\\n- fully handled but intentionally not advertised for a specific surface;\\n- explicitly rejected with a typed `error.surfaced` compatibility event.\\n\\nThe test fails when a future SDK adds an unclassified protocol member.\\n\\n## Phase 1: Claude Code Complete Surface\\n\\n### Inline human input\\n\\nAdd `elicitation` as a first-class `AgentActivityItem`. Pending form elicitations render inline in `Terminal` between the initiating tool call and later activity. The form supports ACP 0.25 property schemas:\\n\\n- strings and free text;\\n- titled `enum`, `oneOf`, and multi-select options;\\n- booleans;\\n- integers and numbers with min/max constraints;\\n- required and optional fields;\\n- field titles and descriptions;\\n- custom-answer fields;\\n- accept, decline, and cancel outcomes.\\n\\nSingle-choice questions render as immediately scannable choices rather than a closed native select. Multi-select questions render as checkboxes. Free text uses the existing shadcn textarea/input controls. The pending form is expanded by default and remains visible after reload.\\n\\nPermission requests become first-class inline actions as well. Every adapter-provided `PermissionOption` is rendered verbatim by stable `optionId`; OmniHarness does not collapse `allow_once`, `allow_always`, `reject_once`, or plan-mode choices into generic Yes/No controls. The action includes the tool title, kind, affected file/command where available, and any diff or plan content.\\n\\nThe sidebar reuses the same field parser, decision model, validation, and translated controls. It is a mirror, not a separate implementation.\\n\\n### Ownership and state\\n\\n- Server authority: pending permission and elicitation records on the live runtime plus pending entries reconstructed from the unified worker stream.\\n- Stable token: [REDACTED] + requestId + toolCallId`.\\n- Ordering: worker stream `seq`.\\n- Terminal states: answered/approved/denied/declined/cancelled/failed.\\n- Draft owner: a dedicated global interaction manager keyed by the stable request token; only the small inline form subscribes to its draft slice.\\n- Mutation ownership: a response may settle only the exact still-pending request token. Late success/error callbacks cannot modify another run or request.\\n- Persistence: pending protocol records and terminal outcome records remain in the worker stream. Draft form values use request-token-keyed `sessionStorage`, survive reload/navigation in the same tab, and clear on settlement, cancellation, sign-out, or expiry. They are never sent to snapshots before submission.\\n- Settlement order: validate ownership, append the terminal outcome to the unified worker stream, update the runtime pending-request projection, then resolve the waiting ACP request. A crash before resolution replays the persisted terminal outcome and never presents the same request as unanswered.\\n\\n### Claude output completeness\\n\\nHandle and expose every feature claimed by the installed Claude adapter:\\n\\n- text and image prompts;\\n- generic tool calls and results;\\n- permissions and `ExitPlanMode` decisions;\\n- `AskUserQuestion` and MCP form elicitation;\\n- edit/write diffs;\\n- TODO and Task plan updates;\\n- Bash interactive/background terminal output and cancellation;\\n- available slash commands;\\n- mode and configuration changes;\\n- session metadata and usage;\\n- client MCP servers;\\n- load, resume, fork, close, and delete session operations supported by the adapter.\\n\\nThe executable Claude coverage matrix names the adapter's concrete built-in projections: Agent/Task, Bash, Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep, WebFetch, WebSearch, TodoWrite, TaskCreate, TaskUpdate, TaskList, TaskGet, ExitPlanMode, AskUserQuestion, and generic/unknown tools. Adapter additions fail the compatibility test until classified.\\n\\n## Phase 2: Full ACP 0.25 Client\\n\\n### Agent-to-client requests\\n\\nImplement every standard request in the ACP 0.25 `AgentRequest` union:\\n\\n- `fs/read_text_file` and `fs/write_text_file` with workspace-bound path validation and named failure events;\\n- `session/request_permission`;\\n- `terminal/create`, `terminal/output`, `terminal/wait_for_exit`, `terminal/kill`, and `terminal/release` using a server-owned terminal manager with bounded output, cancellation, cleanup, and persisted tool metadata;\\n- `elicitation/create` for form and URL modes, including URL completion notifications;\\n- `mcp/connect`, `mcp/message`, and `mcp/disconnect` using connection-scoped ownership and cleanup;\\n- extension requests through an explicit registry. Unknown extensions return method-not-found and emit `acp.compatibility_unsupported`.\\n\\nConnection initialization and capability exchange are explicitly tested before any session/provider/NES operation. JSON-RPC cancellation of pending agent/client requests is accepted throughout initialization, prompting, permissions, elicitations, terminals, MCP, and shutdown.\\n\\n### Agent-to-client notifications\\n\\nHandle all ACP 0.25 notifications:\\n\\n- `session/update`;\\n- `elicitation/complete`;\\n- MCP messages;\\n- registered extension notifications.\\n\\n`elicitation/complete` remains agent-to-client as defined by the installed ACP 0.25 schema: it informs OmniHarness that a URL-mode elicitation completed externally.\\n\\n### Session updates\\n\\nPersist or project every `SessionUpdate` variant:\\n\\n- user, agent, and thought content chunks;\\n- tool calls and updates;\\n- plan, plan update, and plan removed;\\n- available commands;\\n- current mode;\\n- configuration options;\\n- session information;\\n- usage and cost.\\n\\nUnknown discriminators emit a compatibility failure and mark the runtime snapshot degraded instead of disappearing.\\n\\n### Content and tool-call content\\n\\nSupport every ACP content block and tool-call content variant: text, image, audio, embedded text/blob resources, resource links, diffs, terminals, and nested content. Binary payloads are bounded and represented by durable metadata or existing attachment storage, never expanded into unbounded SSE snapshots.\\n\\n### Client capabilities\\n\\nImplement and truthfully negotiate all ACP 0.25 `ClientCapabilities`:\\n\\n- auth environment-variable and terminal flows;\\n- form and URL elicitation;\\n- filesystem read/write;\\n- next-edit document/context capabilities;\\n- structured plan updates;\\n- position encodings;\\n- terminal operations.\\n\\n### Agent capabilities and client-originated methods\\n\\nExpose all ACP 0.25 agent capabilities through typed runtime methods and appropriate product surfaces or scriptable APIs:\\n\\n- authentication, provider listing/selection/disable/logout;\\n- session new/load/list/resume/fork/close/delete;\\n- modes and configuration options;\\n- prompt and cancel;\\n- next-edit start/suggest/close plus document/open/focus/change/save notifications;\\n- MCP-over-ACP messaging;\\n- extension registry.\\n\\nProvider, session, and next-edit operations use Manager-owned client state, stable operation tokens, named events, and full backend error details.\\n\\n## UI Surfaces\\n\\n### Conversation timeline\\n\\nPending human input is placed inline and expanded. It uses the current restrained visual system, clear warning/attention semantics, keyboard-focus rings, and `aria-live` status updates. Settled interactions collapse to compact historical rows that state the outcome without exposing secret values.\\n\\n### Composer\\n\\nAvailable ACP slash commands feed a command menu owned by a dedicated manager. Commands remain scoped to the selected live session and cannot leak across navigation.\\n\\n### Plans and tasks\\n\\nACP plans/tasks render in the conversation as structured progress, distinct from Omni supervisor planning artifacts. Updates merge by stable plan/task ID and sequence; removals are explicit.\\n\\n### Terminal output\\n\\nBash tool rows embed bounded live terminal output and expose stop/cancel only while the corresponding process is active. Terminal resource cleanup is server-owned and observable.\\n\\n### Provider, session, and next-edit surfaces\\n\\nProvider/session operations reuse existing settings and conversation controls where they naturally belong. Next-edit suggestions integrate with project-file viewing rather than creating a fake editor. Scriptable HTTP endpoints remain available for capabilities without a permanently visible control.\\n\\n## Observability\\n\\nAdd named events for:\\n\\n- `acp.request_received`, `acp.request_completed`, `acp.request_failed`;\\n- `acp.compatibility_unsupported`;\\n- `worker.elicitation_requested`, `worker.elicitation_answered`, `worker.elicitation_declined`, `worker.elicitation_cancelled`;\\n- `worker.permission_requested` and terminal permission outcomes;\\n- terminal created/exited/killed/released;\\n- MCP connected/disconnected/failed;\\n- plan updated/removed;\\n- provider/session/NES operations and failures.\\n\\nUser-relevant failures also emit `error.surfaced` with stable codes and run/worker/session ownership.\\n\\n## Error and Security Requirements\\n\\n- Filesystem operations are restricted to the agent session workspace and explicitly attached additional directories.\\n- URL elicitation uses an allowlisted browser/open-URL flow and never auto-completes from an untrusted page.\\n- Terminal commands inherit the worker environment but never expose secret environment values in output metadata.\\n- Terminal services retain at most 1,000 recent lines in memory and append at most 50 KiB of output per tool/turn to the unified stream, with explicit truncation metadata.\\n- MCP connections are bounded per server-side ACP agent session, use connect/message/health timeouts, and close on agent-session cancellation, agent exit, or server shutdown. Browser/SSE disconnects do not own them.\\n- Provider authentication values are never persisted in worker output or frontend state.\\n- Unknown protocol input is preserved in diagnostic previews with size limits, while full secret-bearing payloads are not logged.\\n\\n## Testing\\n\\n### Deterministic tests\\n\\n- Schema compatibility test for every ACP 0.25 union member.\\n- Runtime request/notification tests for every agent-to-client method.\\n- Session-update round-trip tests for every discriminator and content type.\\n- Worker-stream reconstruction tests for pending and settled interactions.\\n- React activity/model tests for elicitation, permissions, plans, commands, terminals, and media.\\n- Mutation ownership tests for navigation, replay, duplicate response, and stale callbacks.\\n- Locale parity tests.\\n- Hot-path tests proving pending forms and terminal updates do not trigger whole-app per-keystroke rendering or unbounded snapshots.\\n\\n### Lifecycle scenarios\\n\\n- Claude asks a multi-choice question; the main conversation displays it; the selected answer reaches Claude; Claude continues.\\n- Reload while a question or permission is pending; the same request reappears exactly once and remains actionable.\\n- Exit-plan-mode options preserve their exact IDs and selected mode.\\n- Disconnect/reconnect replays plan, terminal, permission, and elicitation events without duplication.\\n- Server restart cleans up or reattaches terminal and MCP resources with named decisions.\\n- Unknown future method/update produces an explicit compatibility failure.\\n\\n### Product verification\\n\\nUse the already-running application for browser verification. Verify desktop and narrow/mobile layouts, keyboard-only completion, focus retention, visible loading/error/settled states, and that typing in a pending answer does not repaint the conversation shell. Approval-gated agentic journey testing may be proposed separately; deterministic browser and lifecycle coverage is required regardless.\\n\\n## Acceptance Criteria\\n\\n1. The exact question from session `0abd06014635` renders inline with its four choices and custom-answer field when replayed as a fixture.\\n2. A user can answer every Claude Code permission and elicitation without opening the worker sidebar.\\n3. Every feature claimed by the installed Claude adapter is handled end to end.\\n4. Every ACP 0.25 standard method, notification, session update, content variant, and capability is classified by an executable compatibility test.\\n5. OmniHarness never advertises an incomplete capability.\\n6. Reload, reconnect, recovery, duplication, and stale-response tests pass.\\n7. All user-facing copy is translated through every locale resource.\\n8. Typecheck, lint, focused tests, lifecycle tests, build, and live product verification pass.\\n\\nAcceptance criterion 3 is enforced against the explicit Claude tool/feature matrix above and the adapter's advertised session capabilities, not by prose inspection.\\n# Complete Claude Code and ACP 0.25 Client Implementation Plan\\n\\n> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use ultrapowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.     "text": "---\nname: test-driven-development\ndescription: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n---\n\n# Test-Driven Development (TDD)\n\n## Overview\n\nWrite the test first. Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.\n\n**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.\n\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\n\n## When to Use\n\n**Always:**\n- New features\n- Bug fixes\n- Refactoring\n- Behavior changes\n\n**Exceptions (ask your human partner):**\n- Throwaway prototypes\n- Generated code\n- Configuration files\n\n**Do directly with targeted verification:**\n- tiny color, spacing, copy, or label changes,\n- one-line prompt wording changes,\n- docs-only wording changes,\n- mechanical edits where the expected result is obvious from the diff.\n\nDo not perform a full red-green cycle for a tiny mechanical edit unless it changes behavior or there is a real regression risk. For example, changing a button from gray to blue usually needs the edit plus a targeted visual or code check, not a new failing test.\n\nThinking \"skip TDD just this once\"? Stop. That's rationalization.\n\n## UI-Specific Note\n\nWhen the task is UI work, choose the design system and starting block before writing tests around the intended behavior and rendering. In Ultrapowers, that means `shadcn/ui` plus the closest available ShadCN Block for each new screen, form, or major surface unless the user says otherwise. Inspect https://ui.shadcn.com/blocks first, install or copy the selected block, and then adapt it. Before adding any individual control, check whether `shadcn/ui` already provides a suitable control and use/customize that control first.\n\nFor important app workflows, also consider whether `agentic-user-journey-testing` should be proposed. Do not run that higher-cost black-box test mode without explicit user approval.\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\n```\n\nWrite code before the test? Delete it. Start over.\n\n**No exceptions:**\n- Don't keep it as \"reference\"\n- Don't \"adapt\" it while writing tests\n- Don't look at it\n- Delete means delete\n\nImplement fresh from tests. Period.\n\n## Red-Green-Refactor\n\n```dot\ndigraph tdd_cycle {\n    rankdir=LR;\n    red [label=\"RED\\nWrite failing test\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ffcccc\"];\n    verify_red [label=\"Verify fails\\ncorrectly\", shape=diamond];\n    green [label=\"GREEN\\nMinimal code\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccffcc\"];\n    verify_green [label=\"Verify passes\\nAll green\", shape=diamond];\n    refactor [label=\"REFACTOR\\nClean up\", shape=box, style=filled, fillcolor=\"#ccccff\"];\n    next [label=\"Next\", shape=ellipse];\n\n    red -> verify_red;\n    verify_red -> green [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_red -> red [label=\"wrong\\nfailure\"];\n    green -> verify_green;\n    verify_green -> refactor [label=\"yes\"];\n    verify_green -> green [label=\"no\"];\n    refactor -> verify_green [label=\"stay\\ngreen\"];\n    verify_green -> next;\n    next -> red;\n}\n```\n\n### RED - Write Failing Test\n\nWrite one minimal test showing what should happen.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\ntest('retries failed operations 3 times', async () => {\n  let attempts = 0;\n  const operation = () => {\n    attempts++;\n    if (attempts < 3) throw new Error('fail');\n    return 'success';\n  };\n\n  const result = await retryOperation(operation);\n\n  expect(result).toBe('success');\n  expect(attempts).toBe(3);\n});\n```\nClear name, tests real behavior, one thing\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\ntest('retry works', async () => {\n  const mock = jest.fn()\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error())\n    .mockResolvedValueOnce('success');\n  await retryOperation(mock);\n  expect(mock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(3);\n});\n```\nVague name, tests mock not code\n</Bad>\n\n**Requirements:**\n- One behavior\n- Clear name\n- Real code (no mocks unless unavoidable)\n- Real product behavior is the target. Mocked behavior can never be the final deliverable or proof that the feature works.\n\n### Verify RED - Watch It Fail\n\n**MANDATORY. Never skip.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test fails (not errors)\n- Failure message is expected\n- Fails because feature missing (not typos)\n\n**Test passes?** You're testing existing behavior. Fix test.\n\n**Test errors?** Fix error, re-run until it fails correctly.\n\n### GREEN - Minimal Code\n\nWrite simplest code to pass the test.\n\n<Good>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n    try {\n      return await fn();\n    } catch (e) {\n      if (i === 2) throw e;\n    }\n  }\n  throw new Error('unreachable');\n}\n```\nJust enough to pass\n</Good>\n\n<Bad>\n```typescript\nasync function retryOperation<T>(\n  fn: () => Promise<T>,\n  options?: {\n    maxRetries?: number;\n    backoff?: 'linear' | 'exponential';\n    onRetry?: (attempt: number) => void;\n  }\n): Promise<T> {\n  // YAGNI\n}\n```\nOver-engineered\n</Bad>\n\nDon't add features, refactor other code, or \"improve\" beyond the test.\n\n### Verify GREEN - Watch It Pass\n\n**MANDATORY.**\n\n```bash\nnpm test path/to/test.test.ts\n```\n\nConfirm:\n- Test passes\n- Other tests still pass\n- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\n\n**Test fails?** Fix code, not test.\n\n**Other tests fail?** Fix now.\n\n### REFACTOR - Clean Up\n\nAfter green only:\n- Remove duplication\n- Improve names\n- Extract helpers\n\nKeep tests green. Don't add behavior.\n\n### Repeat\n\nNext failing test for next feature.\n\n## Good Tests\n\n| Quality | Good | Bad |\n|---------|------|-----|\n| **Minimal** | One thing. \"and\" in name? Split it. | `test('validates email and domain and whitespace')` |\n| **Clear** | Name describes behavior | `test('test1')` |\n| **Shows intent** | Demonstrates desired API | Obscures what code should do |\n\n## Why Order Matters\n\n**\"I'll write tests after to verify it works\"**\n\nTests written after code pass immediately. Passing immediately proves nothing:\n- Might test wrong thing\n- Might test implementation, not behavior\n- Might miss edge cases you forgot\n- You never saw it catch the bug\n\nTest-first forces you to see the test fail, proving it actually tests something.\n\n**\"I already manually tested all the edge cases\"**\n\nManual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:\n- No record of what you tested\n- Can't re-run when code changes\n- Easy to forget cases under pressure\n- \"It worked when I tried it\" ≠ comprehensive\n\nAutomated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.\n\n**\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\"**\n\nSunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:\n- Delete and rewrite with TDD (X more hours, high confidence)\n- Keep it and add tests after (30 min, low confidence, likely bugs)\n\nThe \"waste\" is keeping code you can't trust. Working code without real tests is technical debt.\n\n**\"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting\"**\n\nTDD IS pragmatic:\n- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)\n- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)\n- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)\n- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)\n\n\"Pragmatic\" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.\n\n---\nname: verification-before-completion\ndescription: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n---\n\n# Verification Before Completion\n\n## Overview\n\nClaiming work is complete without verification is dishonesty, not efficiency.\n\n**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.\n\n**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n```\nNO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE\n```\n\nIf you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.\n\n## The Gate Function\n\n```\nBEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:\n\n1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?\n2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)\n3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures\n4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?\n   - If NO: State actual status with evidence\n   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence\n5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim\n\nSkip any step = lying, not verifying\n```\n\n## Product-Surface Verification\n\nWhen the work involves an app, UI, or product surface, verification must cover more than whether a single requested feature works.\n\nKeep verification proportional. For tiny mechanical edits, targeted verification is enough: inspect the diff, confirm the changed value/text, and run only the smallest relevant check. Do not escalate to full product-surface verification unless the change can affect behavior, state, navigation, persistence, or the user journey.\n\nAlso verify:\n\n- required functionality is real end-to-end, not a fake component, mock, placeholder, canned response, or fallback path,\n- the primary user stories are actually supported,\n- the primary user journey works end-to-end,\n- any approved agentic user journey tests have been run, or clearly state that they were proposed but not run because approval was not given,\n- return/revisit flows work when they are part of the product,\n- expected empty states are present,\n- expected loading, waiting, or in-progress states are visible,\n- expected error and recovery states are present,\n- expected completed or success states are understandable,\n- expected status-awareness flows are present,\n- the state model is represented correctly,\n- React/server-state invariants are verified when the app uses server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state,\n- persistence behavior for settings, preferences, drafts, and other user-managed state matches the approved design,\n- operational readiness expectations are met for the approved scope,\n- instrumentation and observability exist where the approved scope depends on them,\n- backend or agent workflow decisions emit the planned named events, and a headless/scriptable check can assert the event sequence without relying on UI snapshots,\n- real errors are surfaced rather than silently swallowed or replaced with vague placeholders,\n- onboarding and discoverability work for first-run or empty-state use,\n- risk and trust surfaces are handled well enough to avoid silent failure, ambiguous state, or obvious loss of confidence,\n- when the scope includes a frontend/backend boundary, frontend-visible failures preserve the real error details and stack traces expected by the approved design,\n- baseline expected v1 surfaces and inferred behaviors from the approved story set, spec, or plan are actually present.\n\nWhen the scope includes settings or preferences, also verify:\n\n- each meaningful setting persists or does not persist exactly as designed,\n- the storage location and ownership model match the approved plan,\n- refresh, restart, sign-out, reset, and migration behavior work as intended for the approved scope,\n- settings dialogs avoid redundant section titles, subtitles, explanatory filler, and decorative chrome that does not help the user make a decision.\n\nDo not confuse \"the requested control exists\" with \"the product is usable.\"\n\nFor app, UI, and product-surface work, consider whether an agentic user journey test is the right proof. This mode is approval-gated because it can spend significant tokens: propose it when useful, but only run it if the user explicitly approves.\n\n## Common Failures\n\n| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |\n|-------|----------|----------------|\n| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, \"should pass\" |\n| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |\n| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |\n| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |\n| Meaningful system bug fixed | Learning note plus verification | Fix committed with no durable lesson |\n| React/server state sync works | Stale-response, merge, event-sequence, and ownership tests relevant to the change | UI looked right once |\n| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |\n| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports \"success\" |\n| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |\n| Feature complete | Real end-to-end behavior in the product | Mocked component, canned data, placeholder UI, fallback path |\n\n## Red Flags - STOP\n\n- Using \"should\", \"probably\", \"seems to\"\n- Expressing satisfaction before verification (\"Great!\", \"Perfect!\", \"Done!\", etc.)\n- About to commit/push/PR without verification\n- Trusting agent success reports\n- Accepting \"validated with mocks\" or \"placeholder works\" as completion\n- Relying on partial verification\n- Thinking \"just this once\"\n- Tired and wanting work over\n- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**\n\n## Rationalization Prevention\n\n| Excuse | Reality |\n|--------|---------|\n| \"Should work now\" | RUN the verification |\n| \"I'm confident\" | Confidence ≠ evidence |\n| \"Just this once\" | No exceptions |\n| \"Linter passed\" | Linter ≠ compiler |\n| \"Agent said success\" | Verify independently |\n| \"I'm tired\" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |\n| \"Partial check is enough\" | Partial proves nothing |\n| \"Different words so rule doesn't apply\" | Spirit over letter |\n\n## Key Patterns\n\n**Tests:**\n```\n✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] \"All tests pass\"\n❌ \"Should pass now\" / \"Looks correct\"\n```\n\n**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**\n```\n✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)\n❌ \"I've written a regression test\" (without red-green verification)\n```\n\n**Build:**\n```\n✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] \"Build passes\"\n❌ \"Linter passed\" (linter doesn't check compilation)\n```\n\n**Requirements:**\n```\n✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion\n❌ \"Tests pass, phase complete\"\n```\n\n**Agent delegation:**\n```\n✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state\n❌ Trust agent report\n```\n\n## Why This Matters\n\nFrom 24 failure memories:\n- your human partner said \"I don't believe you\" - trust broken\n- Undefined functions shipped - would crash\n- Missing requirements shipped - incomplete features\n- Time wasted on false completion → redirect → rework\n- Violates: \"Honesty is a core value. If you lie, you'll be replaced.\"\n\n## When To Apply\n\n**ALWAYS before:**\n- ANY variation of success/completion claims\n- ANY expression of satisfaction\n- ANY positive statement about work state\n- Committing, PR creation, task completion\n- Staging, committing, pushing, or opening a PR when the work could include secrets, local environment files, dependencies, caches, build outputs, logs, temporary files, or generated/intermediate artifacts\n- Moving to next task\n- Delegating to agents\n\nWhen a meaningful bug involved architecture, storage, memory, persistence, control planes, background workers, recovery, data loss, security, or a repeated wrong assumption, use `ultrapowers:learning-from-bugs` before claiming the fix is complete.\n\nWhen a React app has server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, SSE/WebSocket streams, background jobs, or persisted runtime state, use `ultrapowers:client-server-state-invariants` to decide which race and synchronization tests are required before claiming completion.\n\nBefore any git publish step, verify `.gitignore` exists for the project and that `git status` does not include secrets, credentials, local environment files, `node_modules`, dependency caches, build outputs, coverage output, logs, temporary files, or intermediate/generated artifacts unless the human explicitly asked to version a specific generated artifact.\n\n**Rule applies to:**\n- Exact phrases\n- Paraphrases and synonyms\n- Implications of success\n- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness\n\n## The Bottom Line\n\n**No shortcuts for verification.**\n\nRun the command. Read the output. THEN claim the result.\n\nThis is non-negotiable.\n---\nname: learning-from-bugs\ndescription: Use when a meaningful bug, regression, architecture flaw, storage issue, memory problem, persistence failure, control-plane failure, or system-level failure has been diagnosed or fixed\n---\n\n# Learning From Bugs\n\nWhen a bug teaches something reusable, capture the lesson before moving on.\n\n## Use This For\n\nUse this after diagnosing or fixing bugs that involve:\n\n- architecture or ownership boundaries,\n- storage, persistence, migrations, caching, or file paths,\n- memory management, leaks, lifecycle, cleanup, or resource pressure,\n- concurrency, queues, retries, resumability, or recovery,\n- control planes, event streams, background workers, or long-running jobs,\n- security, data loss, user trust, or incident-like failures,\n- any bug that took repeated attempts or revealed a wrong mental model.\n\nSkip it for typos, obvious one-line UI mistakes, throwaway test fixture fixes, or purely local churn that will not help future work.\n\n## Required Output\n\nWrite a short learning note to:\n\n`docs/superpowers/learnings/YYYY-MM-DD-<bug-or-lesson-slug>.md`\n\nCreate the directory if needed.\n\nUse this shape:\n\n```markdown\n# [Lesson Title]\n\n**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD\n**Context:** [project, feature, or subsystem]\n**Symptom:** [what failed]\n**Root Cause:** [the real cause, not the symptom]\n**Fix:** [what changed]\n**Verification:** [commands, tests, or checks that proved it]\n**Prevention:** [what future agents should do differently]\n**Skill/Doc Updates:** [nearby skills or docs updated, or why no general update was needed]\n```\n\nKeep it concrete. Future agents should be able to read the note and avoid the same mistake.\n\n## Update The System When The Lesson Generalizes\n\nIf the bug reveals a reusable rule, update the nearest skill or defaults doc in the same change. Examples:\n\n- debugging lesson -> `systematic-debugging`\n- verification gap -> `verification-before-completion`\n- control-plane or eventing lesson -> `instrumenting-control-planes`\n- React lifecycle or routing lesson -> `building-react-apps`\n- planning omission -> `writing-plans` or `brainstorming`\n\nDo not bury a general lesson only in a project note. The learning note records the case; the skill update changes future behavior.\n\n## Completion Gate\n\nBefore saying a meaningful bug is fixed, check:\n\n1. Is this lesson reusable?\n2. Is there a learning note?\n3. Did any skill or defaults doc need an update?\n4. Did verification prove the fix?\n\nIf the answer to 1 is yes, do not skip the note. If the answer to 3 is no, say why in the note.\n"
  }
]
````

const patch = "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness-recovery-2026-08-01-0045/build-restored-candidate.sql\n@@\n INSERT INTO worker_counters(run_id, next_number, updated_at)\n@@\n FROM versions\n WHERE version_rank = 1;\n+\n+-- Some direct-conversation plan table pages were securely cleared even though\n+-- their run rows survived. Recreate only the missing parent rows so every\n+-- recovered conversation remains loadable and the database is referentially\n+-- valid. The title and full transcript remain owned by the run and worker\n+-- stream; this fallback path is used only for the recovered plan reference.\n+INSERT INTO plans(id, path, status, created_at, updated_at)\n+SELECT\n+  r.plan_id,\n+  COALESCE(\n+    r.spec_path,\n+    r.artifact_plan_path,\n+    'vibes/ad-hoc/recovered-' || r.id || '.md'\n+  ),\n+  CASE\n+    WHEN r.status IN ('done', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'stopped', 'completed') THEN 'done'\n+    ELSE 'running'\n+  END,\n+  r.created_at,\n+  r.updated_at\n+FROM runs AS r\n+LEFT JOIN plans AS p ON p.id = r.plan_id\n+WHERE p.id IS NULL;\n+\n+-- Old event rows may refer to workers or plan items whose table pages were\n+-- securely cleared before recovery. Preserve the event itself while removing\n+-- only the dangling optional reference.\n+UPDATE execution_events\n+SET worker_id = NULL\n+WHERE worker_id IS NOT NULL\n+  AND worker_id NOT IN (SELECT id FROM workers);\n+\n+UPDATE execution_events\n+SET plan_item_id = NULL\n+WHERE plan_item_id IS NOT NULL\n+  AND plan_item_id NOT IN (SELECT id FROM plan_items);\n \n COMMIT;\n*** End Patch";
text(await tools.apply_patch(patch));

```

'';\n  for (const line of lines) {\n    if (!line.trim()) continue;\n    const message = JSON.parse(line);\n    if (message.method === 'initialize') {\n      const pathEntries = (process.env.PATH || || null;\n      append({\n        method: message.method,\n        params: message.params,\n        argv: process.argv.slice(2),\n        codexManagedConfigPath: process.env.CODEX_MANAGED_CONFIG_PATH result: { sessionId: 'session-1' } });\n    }\n    if (message.method === 'session/set_mode') {\n      append({ method: message.method, params: message.params });\n      write({ jsonrpc: '2.0', id: result: {} });\n    }\n    if (message.method === 'session/prompt') {\n      append({ method: message.method, params: message.params });\n      if (process.env.FAKE_ACP_STDERR_ON_PROMPT) {\n Open the event log for the run: `GET /api/events/log?runId=<id>`.\n2. Look for the named event corresponding to X.\n3. If it is present: the server did the thing. The bug is in the UI or in\n   the user's mental model.\n4. If it is absent: the server did not do the thing. Find the code path that\n   *would* have emitted it, and find the early-return / swallowed error /\n   silent branch that prevented it. Fix that, and emit the appropriate event\n   (success or failure).\n5. If the code path doesn't emit anything in either branch: that is the bug.\n   Add the event per \"How to add a new server-side decision.\"\n\nEvery reported bug should land at one of these four answers. If the answer is\n\"I don't know, the server is a black box\", we have failed Rule 1.\n\n---\n\n## Worker conversation content vs. lifecycle events\n\nThese two surfaces are distinct and must stay distinct.\n\n- **Lifecycle events** (`worker.spawned`, `worker.status`,\n  `worker.terminal`, `recovery.*`, etc.) live in the ring buffer and on\n  the SSE stream. They are typed in `src/server/events/named-events.ts`\n  and emitted via `emitNamedEvent`. Their purpose is observability:\n  what decision did the server make.\n- **Worker conversation content** (what the worker said, what tools\n  it called, what the user typed, what the supervisor steered) lives\n  in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker. The\n  canonical content cursor is `entry.seq`, not the global SSE id. SSE\n  emits a `worker.entry_appended` named event carrying only `{\n  workerId, seq }`; the entry body is fetched via\n  `GET /api/workers/:workerId/entries?afterSeq=`. See\n  `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.\n\nTwo cursors deliberately: the global SSE id covers liveness/resync;\n`entry.seq` covers content. SSE frames are wake-up hints, never the\nauthority on content.\n\n## Open follow-ups\n\n- Backfill named events at every existing decision site (see implementation\n  plan).\n- Adopt SSE-spec compliance in the UI client (snapshot bootstrap +\n  `Last-Event-ID` resume). Today the UI just refetches a snapshot, which\n  works but is wasteful.\n- Audit every server `try/catch` and every early-`return` for missing\n  events. This is a one-time sweep; new code is held to the rules above\n  going forward.\n- Build out the headless chaos harness and the scenario catalog.\n\n---\n\n## Audit log\n\n### `try/catch` sweep — first pass\n\nWalked every `} catch {` in `src/server/**` and `src/app/api/**`. Findings:\n\n- **Legitimate silent fallbacks** (kept as-is, comment explains why):\n  `restart-control.ts` process-liveness probes (`process.kill(pid, 0)`),\n  `restart-control.ts:264` lsof-empty no-listener case,\n  `conversations/create.ts:257` opportunistic snapshot persist (the\n  function's user-visible state still comes from the `askAgent`\n  response, not the dropped snapshot),\n  `planning/review.ts:313` reviewer snapshot persist (same shape),\n  parse-error fallbacks in `plans/readiness-pipeline.ts`,\n  `planning/artifacts.ts`, `auth/guards.ts`, `projects/canonicalize.ts`.\n  Each catches an internal probe that cannot affect user-visible state.\n  These match the rare-exception carve-out for idempotency / probe\n  catches.\n\n- **Bugs that were already fixed in phase 2** (no remaining silent\n  failure in this category):\n  - Plan-review leftover-state silent throw → now emits\n    `plan.review.blocked` + `error.surfaced` (`planning/review.ts`).\n  - Recovery exhaustion silent cap → now emits `recovery.gave_up` +\n    `error.surfaced` (`runs/recovery-incidents.ts`).\n  - Delete conversation FK 500 → now returns 409 with\n    `conversation.delete_failed` + `error.surfaced` (`api/runs/[id]`).\n\n- **No new offenders found in the first pass.** New code is held to the\n  rule via the AGENTS.md entry; reviewer should reject silent catches on\n  state-mutating paths.\n\nFuture audit passes should look at:\n- ~~Early-return sites in `supervisor/observer.ts` (decision branches).~~\n  **Done.** All 16 `stopRunObserver` call sites now pass a typed\n  `reason` (`run_terminated`, `run_failed`, `cwd_mismatch`,\n  `snapshot_invalid`, `quota_exhausted`, `fatal_bridge_error`,\n  `explicit`). `supervisor.stopped` is emitted from inside\n  `stopRunObserver` itself, guarded by `wasActive` so bare stop calls\n  on never-started runs don't spam the stream. Scenario:\n  `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/supervisor-stopped.test.ts`.\n- ~~Every `if (!latestRun)` short-circuit that stops the observer.~~\n  Covered by the above — those now emit `supervisor.stopped` with\n  reason `run_terminated`.\n\n### Claude model gateway lifecycle\n\nThe managed Claude model gateway follows the same control-plane rules as workers and\nsessions. One server singleton owns its redacted status, monotonic revision, current\noperation, OAuth polling, and model cache. The operational API and canonical event\nsnapshot expose that same status; clients must not infer readiness from cached models.\n\nInstall, start, stop, OAuth, and model-refresh decisions emit typed\n`claude_gateway.*` events on the existing ring buffer. Managed mode adopts or starts only a process whose saved PID\nand command identity prove OmniHarness ownership. External mode performs bounded\nreadiness requests and never signals a process. Stop disables future autostart and\npreserves binaries, configuration, logs, and provider credentials.\n\n### Scenario catalog (`tests/lifecycle/scenarios/`)\n\n| Scenario | Asserts |\n|---|---|\n| `end-to-end-events` | Snapshot bootstrap + tail + event ordering |\n| `sse-resume` | `Last-Event-ID` replays the gap after a mid-stream drop |\n| `restart-resync` | `stream.resync_required` fires after a server restart |\n| `chaos-reconnect-storm` | Seeded high-drop-rate chaos doesn't lose events |\n| `plan-review-blocked` | Leftover-state throws → 409 + named events on the wire |\n| `delete-conversation-fk` | FK fail → 409 + named events, not a raw 500 |\n| `recovery-exhaustion` | Cap reached → `recovery.gave_up` + `error.surfaced` in order |\n| `worker-reattach` | Observer's revive branch emits `worker.reattached` |\n| `session-types` | Direct / planning / implementation conversation creation |\n| `conversation-continuation` | Mid-conversation SSE drop + reconnect; nothing lost |\n| `plan-improvement-flow` | Review start + worker spawn + restart-resync end-to-end |\n| `real-restart` | **Real subprocess** kill+respawn: sqlite persists, ring resets, client gets `stream.resync_required` |\n| `direct-mode-rerun` | User pressing \"re-run\" — same content sent twice persists as two rows in order |\n| `flaky-network` | Seeded HTTP flake: server stays consistent, deterministic across seed |\n| `worker-spawn-failure` | Bridge `spawnAgent` rejection now emits `error.surfaced(worker.spawn.failed)` instead of going silent |\n| `supervisor-stopped` | Every observer stop site emits `supervisor.stopped` with a typed reason (`run_terminated`, `run_failed`, `cwd_mismatch`, `snapshot_invalid`, `quota_exhausted`, `fatal_bridge_error`, `explicit`) |\n\n### Real protocol bug found via the harness\n\nWhile wiring `recovery-exhaustion`, the harness caught a real bug in the\n"
